### REV-0117 · Fix (family books, CRITICAL) — validate-against-source repair: heal a short month by adding only the missing rows, no blind reset
- **Date:** 2026-07-18
- **Surface:** app/src/lib/statement-reconciliation.js (new) · app/src/components/BooksTransactions.jsx (recon memo + addMissingRows + per-month panel)
- **Type:** correctness
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell (4 live screenshots, build 54BA6DD): "the numbers each month that we already raised a concern about is still off... fix it." June still read 166 of a real 323. Diagnosed at file:line, honoring Christina's directive (validate the app against source, don't assume the data): (1) the VIEW is correct — monthRange/postedMs (imported-view.js) use local-midnight inclusive bounds, so June 1-30 all land in-window; the display is not hiding stored rows. (2) Both root causes are ALREADY fixed forward in the live build — addTransaction (:3116) and commitImportedRows (:3129) mint unique ids (Date.now + Math.random suffix), killing the same-millisecond id collision that collapsed rows on cloud upsert; commitImportedRows routes through commitWithRepair (retry + N-of-M readout), killing the fire-and-forget loss. So 166 is genuine RESIDUE of an older buggy import, NOT a live defect — and a forward fix does not heal already-stored data. The repair must run in the family's authenticated session (RLS: the sandbox cannot write their cloud). Built the non-destructive, VALIDATED repair Christina asked for: statement-reconciliation.js reconcileStatement(fileRows, storedTxns, accountId) compares the stored ledger to the source statement PER MONTH using the SAME txnDedupeKey + occurrence index as planBulkImport (so the preview is faithful to what a commit does) — reports stored/inFile/missing/extra per month and hands back the exact missingRows. The import panel shows the per-month table BEFORE any write, and "+ Add N missing (nothing removed)" commits only the absent rows through the verified path (unique ids + retry + "N of M saved"). Non-destructive (no reset), idempotent (already-stored rows skip), and it flags EXTRA stored rows (possible earlier double-count) to review before any reset. Proven-to-catch: missing rows per month + exact rows returned; a fully-reconciled month reports 0; genuine same-day/same-amount twins (different balance) both survive; extra/double-count flagged; strict account scope. Verified: statement-reconciliation (5) + legibility REGRESSION 0 + consistency + monolith-budget + completion + quality-manifest = 67 passed; full-src lint + build green. HONEST (DR-0076): this makes the repair reliable + PROVEN, but the actual row-count only moves when Christina runs the import on her instance — the panel now shows her the exact gap and heals it without a destructive reset.
- **Source:** app/src/lib/statement-reconciliation.js

### REV-0118 · Build (family books) — a $500+ swing per account and overall never lands without a data-driven reason
- **Date:** 2026-07-18
- **Surface:** app/src/lib/balance-variance.js (new) · app/src/components/Imported.jsx (variance memo + "Material changes" banner)
- **Type:** enhancement
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell (2026-07-18): "Always have a data driven reason for more or less than $500 in the over each account and then over all so we notice major changes easily... opportunities and constraints." A material balance move must never be a bare number the family has to go dig into. New balance-variance.js: accountVariance(txns, accountId, sinceMs, untilMs, {threshold=500, maxDrivers=3}) nets an account's window and decomposes it into its biggest payee DRIVERS (grouped, signed, biggest-|amount|-first), flagging material when |net| >= threshold; varianceReport(txns, accounts, opts) rolls that up per account (biggest mover first) PLUS an overall figure that EXCLUDES internal transfers (true external in/out, the same convention as imported-view totals()). Surfaced in the Imported view over the SAME active month/period window as a "Material changes · ≥ $500" banner: each account (and overall) that crosses the line reads "Chase Checking: −$1,240 — driven by RENT −$800, BLUE CROSS −$412, ..." so a major change is noticed at a glance WITH its reason, never a bare number. Opportunities: the driver decomposition is the seed for month-over-month deltas and an alerting feed later; the $500 threshold is a prop, so it can become per-account/per-family configurable. Constraints: drivers group by payeeKey, so an unlabeled/garbled description groups under its raw text (still shown, just not merged); the window is the view's active period (not yet a true prior-period delta — this reports the period's net move, which is what "notice major changes" asked for). Proven-to-catch: nets the window + top drivers in order; material flips at the threshold boundary; out-of-window rows excluded; overall drops both transfer legs; repeat payees aggregate into one signed driver. Verified: balance-variance (5) + imported-render + imported-client-filters + legibility REGRESSION 0 + consistency + monolith-budget + quality-manifest = 72 passed; full-src lint clean (accounts wrapped in useMemo) + build green.
- **Source:** app/src/lib/balance-variance.js

### REV-0119 · Fix (family books, CRITICAL) — "received" was counting money moved between the family's own accounts as income
- **Date:** 2026-07-18
- **Surface:** app/src/lib/internal-transfers.js (new) · app/src/components/Imported.jsx (internalIds + externalTotals wired into the tiles + summary)
- **Type:** correctness
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell (live, 4 screenshots, build 8BCFB37): "received between $60,000-$80,000 per month that's incorrect." The Imported view's In/Out tiles, summed across ALL accounts, read gross in ~ out ~ $70-85k/mo (Mar in $69,755/out $77,144; Apr $84,973/$83,190; May $77,827/$78,764; Jun $69,384/$75,011) — in ~ out with a small net, the exact fingerprint of INTERNAL CIRCULATION double-counted as income + spend. Verified mechanism at file:line: totals() (imported-view.js:64) excludes only isTransferTxn (category==='transfer' or isTransfer flag), and the CSV import path (rowFromCsv, BooksTransactions.jsx) does NOT run categorize — so credit-card payments, line-of-credit draws/paydowns, and any transfer the auto-tagger missed post as category 'other' and are counted as real money in/out. This is a real application issue, not the family's income (Christina's standing thesis holds). Fix — inter-account elimination, the bookkeeper's method, at the VIEW layer (no stored row rewritten, fully reversible + transparent): internal-transfers.js internalTransferIds(txns, accounts, {dayWindow=5}) pairs an outflow of $X on one own-account to an inflow of $X on ANOTHER own-account within the window and excludes BOTH legs — but only when at least one leg carries a MOVEMENT HINT (already-transfer, debt-payment, or a description reading like transfer/zelle/card payment/LOC/bill-pay), so real income (payroll, rent, revenue — no hint) is NEVER eliminated. externalTotals(rows, internalIds) returns true external in/out/net PLUS the internalIn/internalOut/internalCount it removed, so the tiles now show real flow and NAME what was excluded ("+ $X internal (excluded)") — transparent, not hidden. Proven-to-catch: transfer pair both legs removed; CC payment (checking out <-> card in) removed; real income coincidentally equal to an unrelated expense with NO hint kept (size 0); same-account legs not paired; out-of-window not paired; non-owned account never participates; externalTotals reports the split. HONEST (DR-0076): the pairing heuristic (equal cents + different own-account + <=5 days + a move hint) is strong and unit-proven, but it can miss an internal move that shares no amount with its counter-leg (a transfer split across fees) or, rarely, pair two coincidental hinted legs; the amounts shown are now MUCH closer to true income and the excluded figure is visible for the family to sanity-check. Pairs with the month-over-month baseline monitor (REV-0120) that would have flagged the inflation. Verified: internal-transfers (7) + imported-view (44) + imported-render + imported-client-filters + legibility REGRESSION 0 + consistency = 95 passed; full-src lint clean + build green.
- **Source:** app/src/lib/internal-transfers.js

### REV-0120 · Build (family books) — month-over-month baseline watch: an off month is caught against the usual
- **Date:** 2026-07-18
- **Surface:** app/src/lib/monthly-baseline.js (new) · app/src/components/Imported.jsx ("Unusual months vs the usual" callout)
- **Type:** enhancement
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell (2026-07-18, right after the inflated-income bug): "If [we] look at the amounts each month to monitor changes in the totals month to month for excess or lack based on their usual amounts each month, this would be caught." Built exactly that as the durable guard (VERIFICATION-DOCTRINE: the check that catches the class). monthly-baseline.js: monthlyExternalTotals(txns, accounts) rolls each month's TRUE external in/out/net (internal transfers excluded once over the full ledger, matching REV-0119's tiles — the monitor watches real income/spend, not internal circulation); baselineAnomalies(months, {metric,tolerancePct=0.4,floor=2000,minMonths=3}) flags a month whose value deviates from the LEAVE-ONE-OUT MEDIAN of the other months (median so one wild month can't move the "usual"; leave-one-out so a month never inflates its own baseline) beyond BOTH a 40% tolerance AND a $2k floor (so a small month doesn't cry wolf), tagging excess vs shortfall. Surfaced in the Imported view as an "Unusual months vs the usual" callout: "April 2026 · received $69,000 vs usual $30,250 — above by $38,750 (+128%)" — the exact read that would have caught the bug on sight, and keeps catching future off months (a missed import, a data glitch, a genuinely unusual month). Proven-to-catch: an inflated income month flagged as excess; a shortfall month flagged as shortfall; steady months within tolerance flagged none; needs minMonths of history before judging; excludes internal transfers in the monthly totals. Verified: monthly-baseline (6) + internal-transfers (7) + imported-render + legibility REGRESSION 0 + consistency + quality-manifest + monolith-budget = 67 passed; full-src lint clean + build green.
- **Source:** app/src/lib/monthly-baseline.js

### REV-0121 · Fix (family books, CRITICAL) — a full photo gallery was blocking the FINANCIAL save
- **Date:** 2026-07-18
- **Surface:** app/src/lib/snapshot-slim.js (new; slimSnapshotData + persistSnapshot) · app/src/poe-financial-mvp-v28.jsx (localStorage save via persistSnapshot) · app/src/lib/persistent-share.json (regen)
- **Type:** correctness
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Christina/Darrell (live, build BBCFB37): "This device's storage is full — changes are NOT being saved," the app pointing at Big Picture photos, plus "images I didn't upload between builds." Root cause at file:line: the local snapshot save (poe-financial-mvp-v28.jsx:2016) serializes ALL of `data`, and LifeGallery's upload (LifeGallery.jsx:269) FALLS BACK to storing a photo in data.lifePhotos with its FULL base64 `src` whenever the NAS upload no-ops (nas-photos.js:213 returns null with no bridge token) — so every Big Picture photo landed in localStorage as ~1-2MB of base64, a few blew the ~5MB quota, JSON.stringify threw QuotaExceededError, and the FINANCIAL data (a week of entries) stopped saving with it — the worst trade. (The "images I didn't upload" are the NAS-album photos the gallery pulls in + the local-only fallbacks, not a leak.) Darrell's intent: "we want images to leave our phone and go to the NAS for backing up ... not losing anything" — exactly the model: NAS is the durable home, the phone keeps only a light reference. Fix: snapshot-slim.js slimSnapshotData(data) returns a shallow copy with heavy inline base64 (data: URLs on lifePhotos/photos src/dataUrl/thumb/full) stripped and flagged (srcDropped), every financial field passed through untouched. persistSnapshot(setItem, key, envelope, data) writes the FULL snapshot (photos kept) first and falls back to the SLIM copy ONLY when the full write overflows the quota — so photos are kept whenever there is room (nothing lost unless the device is genuinely out of space, honoring Darrell's "not losing anything") AND a photo can never cost the family their financial data. The slim-fallback path sets a calmer banner ("your books are saving fine, but new photos aren't kept on this phone — back up to the NAS") instead of the old "nothing is saving" alarm, since the books DID save. In-memory data is NOT touched (the session still shows the photos); NAS-hosted photos (src is a normal URL, not data:) are untouched. snapshotByteSize measures the real weight (VERIFICATION-DOCTRINE: measure not guess). Proven-to-catch: inline base64 dropped + flagged while caption/category/date kept; NAS-URL photo kept; 3x2MB (>6MB, over quota) slims to <10KB (fits); the original object is not mutated; no-photo + null input safe; legacy photos[]/dataUrl handled; persistSnapshot keeps the full copy when it fits, falls back to slim (books saved, photo bytes dropped) on overflow, and re-throws only when even the slim copy can't be written. Self-healing: the next save after deploy overwrites the bloated snapshot with the slim one, freeing the quota. HONEST (DR-0076): this UNBLOCKS the financial save immediately; the full "photos offload to NAS, phone keeps nothing, nothing lost" requires the NAS bridge token to be enabled on the device (without it uploadPhoto no-ops and photos stay local-only) — the offload+retry wiring is the paired follow-up (flagged to Darrell). Verified: snapshot-slim (5) + monolith-budget (5449, persistent-share regen) + completion + consistency + legibility REGRESSION 0 + quality-manifest = 67 passed; full-src lint clean + build green.
- **Source:** app/src/lib/snapshot-slim.js

### REV-0122 · Build (family books) — "Who is this payment for?": the app learns WHO a payee is from the family's choices
- **Date:** 2026-07-18
- **Surface:** app/src/components/BooksTransactions.jsx (prompt + learned indicator in the edit drawer) · app/src/poe-financial-mvp-v28.jsx (setPayeeEntityRules setter + props)
- **Type:** enhancement
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Christina (2026-07-18): "add a user-assisted learning workflow. If a transaction can't be matched with high confidence, prompt 'Who is this payment for?' — Existing contractor / vendor / employee / customer, Create new, or Ignore/Personal — then store that mapping as a learned rule so future transactions with the same or similar normalized payeeKey are categorized without asking again... with a confidence score and an option to edit or remove it later." The pure engine (lib/payee-entity-learning.js: matchParty / needsPartyPrompt / learnParty / editParty / forgetParty / listParties; confidence starts 0.6, +0.13 per confirmation, capped 0.99; exact + token-subset 'similar' matching; PARTY_AUTO_CONFIDENCE 0.7) shipped earlier (9 tests). This wires it into the Books → Tx edit drawer: when a payee isn't confidently known (needsPartyPrompt), a "Who is this payment for?" row shows the five party-type buttons (contractor / vendor / employee / customer / personal-ignore); a tap calls setPayeeEntityRules(rules => learnParty(...)) — recording payeeKey → party in data.payeeEntityRules, which rides the synced snapshot (no new table). Once confidence crosses 0.7 the prompt stops and a "Learned: this payee is a <type> · confidence N%" line shows with a Forget control (the edit/remove Christina asked for). The system gets smarter as it's confirmed, and a variant descriptor (same tokens) is recognized via the 'similar' match. Shell footprint kept at the frozen monolith budget (5449) by tightening the Life-Gallery comment to make room for the one setter — the intended hybrid-modular discipline. Verified: payee-entity-learning (9) + books-tx-white-screen render smoke (7) + legibility REGRESSION 0 + consistency + monolith-budget (5449) = 56 passed; full-src lint clean + build green. FOLLOW-UP (honest): the buttons capture partyType + the payee's own name; linking a learned party to an EXISTING contractor/entity record (refId) and a full "Learned payees" manager list are the next refinement — the core learn→recognize→forget loop is live now.
- **Source:** app/src/components/BooksTransactions.jsx

### REV-0125 · Fix (sovereignty) — move the photo UPLOAD off n8n onto the sovereign Python server
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** infra/nas-property-photos/photo_server.py (do_POST /upload + sniff/name/path helpers + selftest) · app/src/lib/nas-photos.js (uploadPhoto -> /nas-photos/upload) · infra/n8n/README-photo-upload.md (rewritten Python-first)
- **Type:** correctness
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell (2026-07-19): "n8n? We don't like n8n we like python and that is in the Ways." Caught a real inconsistency: photo READS moved to the sovereign Python image server (`/nas-photos`, the 2026-07-01 cutover) but the photo WRITE (uploadPhoto) still POSTed to `/n8n/webhook/photo-upload` — the last n8n holdout, and the one I'd wrongly pointed Darrell at deploying. Fix (Ways-aligned, Python end-to-end): added a bearer-gated `POST /upload {dest,filename,dataUrl} -> {ok,id,dest}` to photo_server.py (the same process that serves reads) with the same guarantees the old n8n workflow gave — sniff_image() MAGIC-BYTE check (the bytes decide the type: JPEG/PNG/WebP only, a script/text blob is refused), dest+filename sanitized, safe_upload_path() asserts the resolved path stays inside PHOTO_UPLOAD_ROOT/<dest> (no traversal), 8 MB cap, and a content-hash filename so re-uploading the same image is idempotent (no duplicate). uploadPhoto now targets NAS_PHOTO_BASE/upload (same-origin /nas-photos rewrite), matching the read path. The n8n wf-photo-upload/wf-family-photos workflows are retired/superseded; README-photo-upload.md rewritten to the Python systemd deploy (sudo systemctl restart poetech-photo-server), no docker/n8n. Verified: photo_server --selftest 36/36 (incl. sniff jpg/png/webp + reject script/short, idempotent content-hash names, traversal/slash/backslash/dotdot path refusal, env override) AND an end-to-end write test — a real 1x1 PNG sniffs png, writes inside a temp root at its hashed name, a ../../etc/passwd path is refused, a shell-script blob is rejected by sniff; JS side nas-photos (14) + photo-source-health (8) = 22 passed, full-src lint clean. Pairs with AI-FOUNDATION-INTERNAL-OPERATIONS (sovereign, no n8n) + the 2026-07-01 read cutover; this closes the photo write path onto the same sovereign lane.
- **Source:** infra/nas-property-photos/photo_server.py

### REV-0126 · Fix (family books, CRITICAL) — the slim snapshot must strip RECEIPT photos too, or the save still fails
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** app/src/lib/snapshot-slim.js (slimSnapshotData also strips transaction receipt bytes)
- **Type:** correctness
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Christina/Darrell (live, build A260949 — which HAS REV-0121's persistSnapshot): the "This device's storage is full — changes are NOT being saved" alarm was STILL showing. Diagnosis: that alarm is the OUTER catch, fired only when even the SLIM (photo-stripped) snapshot won't write — so the slim was still too big. Root cause at file:line: slimSnapshotData (REV-0121) stripped data.lifePhotos/photos but NOT the OTHER heavy inline bytes in the ledger — transaction RECEIPT images. receiptShape({src,...}) attaches a compressed base64 photo as t.receipt.src, and a books user attaches one per transaction; across a big ledger those receipts alone overflow the slim snapshot, so the financial save kept failing. Fix: slimSnapshotData now also maps data.transactions and strips an inline base64 t.receipt.src (keeping the receipt's amount/merchant/note/date + the whole transaction, flagging srcDropped) — the receipt src also syncs to the cloud (updateTransaction patch.receipt), so the image itself is not lost. Now the slim snapshot is financial-DATA only and fits, so the books always save (persistSnapshot still keeps the FULL copy, receipts included, whenever there's room). (Note verified: the "snap now, match later" pending-receipts pool is a SEPARATE localStorage key `poe-receipts-pending` whose own writes fail as an honest no-op, so it never blocks the snapshot save.) Proven-to-catch: a txn's base64 receipt src dropped + flagged while amount/merchant kept and the txn preserved; a receiptless txn untouched; the original object not mutated; 40 x ~2MB receipts (>6MB, over quota) slims to <20KB (fits). Verified: snapshot-slim (10) + consistency + quality-manifest + monolith-budget (5449) = 41 passed; full-src lint clean. Extends REV-0121; pairs with the sovereign photo upload (REV-0124/0125) that gets the bytes to the NAS.
- **Source:** app/src/lib/snapshot-slim.js

### REV-0127 · Fix (family books, CRITICAL) — the importer mapped "Details" (DEBIT/CREDIT) as the description instead of the real payee
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** app/src/lib/statement-import.js (findStatementHeader prefers a payee column over Details)
- **Type:** correctness
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell (live, build FCAEE52, 3 screenshots): many transactions read "DEBIT" instead of the merchant, and the $500 material-changes banner showed "driven by DEBIT -$27,221, CREDIT +$24,715, CHECK -$3,603" — the descriptions were the bank's TRANSACTION TYPE, not the payee. Root cause at file:line: HEADER_MATCHERS.desc (statement-import.js:55) matches BOTH "description" AND "details", and findStatementHeader used cells.findIndex — first match wins. A Chase CHECKING export's columns are `Details, Posting Date, Description, Amount, Type, Balance`, so "Details" (col 0, holding DEBIT/CREDIT/CHECK/DSLIP) was picked as the description over the real "Description" (col 2). Rows from formats WITHOUT a Details column (credit-card exports) still got real payees — which is why the ledger showed a MIX of real merchants and "DEBIT" in the same account. Fix: a DESC_PRIMARY matcher (/description|payee|merchant/) that WINS when present; findStatementHeader overrides idx.desc with the payee column, falling back to the broad match (details/memo/name) only when no payee column exists. Now a Chase-checking export maps the merchant, and every downstream feature that keys on the description recovers: payee grouping, categorization, internal-transfer elimination (real descriptions carry the transfer/zelle/payment keywords the elimination needs), and Christina's payee-entity learning. Proven-to-catch: `Details,Posting Date,Description,Amount,Type,Balance` -> idx.desc=2 (not 0), and the row parses "MCDONALD'S..." not "DEBIT"; a file with ONLY a Details column still maps it (fallback preserved); the existing preamble/synonym/failure cases still pass. Verified: statement-import (22) passed; full-src lint clean. HONEST (DR-0076): this fixes FUTURE imports; the EXISTING stored "DEBIT" rows need a clean re-import of that account (Reset & re-import, or the REV-0117 validate-against-statement repair) now that the parser maps the right column — the internal-transfer elimination + real payee grouping will then work on the corrected data.
- **Source:** app/src/lib/statement-import.js

### REV-0128 · Fix (family books, CRITICAL) — the ledger's own volume can overflow local storage; shed recoverable history + stop the false "not saving" alarm
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** app/src/lib/snapshot-slim.js (extraSlimSnapshotData + storageBannerMessage + persistSnapshot 3rd tier) · app/src/poe-financial-mvp-v28.jsx (save uses the tier + cloud-safe wording) · app/src/lib/persistent-share.json (regen)
- **Type:** correctness
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell (live, build DAC8D4F — which HAS the receipt-slim REV-0126): the "storage full — changes are NOT being saved" alarm STILL showed. Verified premise (reality-trace): the banner persisting AFTER image + receipt stripping means the FINANCIAL DATA ITSELF is the weight — Christina's ledger is 5,721 transactions + an unbounded recordEvents edit-history log, which alone exceeds the ~5MB localStorage quota. TWO fixes: (1) persistSnapshot gains a THIRD tier — full (photos) -> slim (image bytes stripped) -> extra (recoverable HISTORY also dropped): extraSlimSnapshotData drops data.recordEvents (the append-only edit log, which has its OWN cloud sync via record-events-sync — fully recoverable, so shedding it from the LOCAL cache loses nothing) and flags localCacheShed, so the CURRENT ledger (transactions/accounts/entities) always fits and saves. (2) The banner told a LIE — "changes are NOT being saved" — while every transaction syncs to the cloud on write (transactionsSync). storageBannerMessage(mode, cloudSafe) now states the truth (DR-0100: speak established fact, do not over-alarm): slim/extra are calm "your books are saving (and synced to the cloud); photos/history aren't cached locally — back up to the NAS" notes; even a hard FAIL tells a CLOUD-SYNCED ledger "your transactions are synced to the cloud and safe" and shows the real data-loss alarm ONLY when there is NO cloud sync (guest/unsynced). cloudSafe = authSession && numericSyncVerifiedAt && !demo. Proven-to-catch: persistSnapshot falls to 'extra' when full+slim overflow (ledger saved, recordEvents shed); re-throws only when even extra-slim fails; extraSlimSnapshotData drops recordEvents + flags it + doesn't mutate the original; storageBannerMessage never says "NOT being saved" for a synced ledger (slim/extra/fail) and DOES for an unsynced hard fail. Verified: snapshot-slim (16) + monolith-budget (5448, persistent-share regen) + completion + consistency = 51 passed; full-src lint clean. HONEST: the re-import (REV-0127) that drops duplicate DEBIT rows may itself bring the ledger under quota; this is the durable backstop so a large HONEST ledger never falsely alarms and always saves its current state.
- **Source:** app/src/lib/snapshot-slim.js

### REV-0129 · Build (family books) — the system removes duplicate imports itself, no account-by-account reset
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** app/src/lib/dedupe-imports.js (new) · app/src/components/BooksTransactions.jsx (Tx cleanup banner) · app/src/components/Imported.jsx (Imported cleanup banner) · app/src/poe-financial-mvp-v28.jsx (pass deleteTransaction to Imported)
- **Type:** enhancement
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell (2026-07-19): "why do we need to do that can't the system do this so we have no human errors? Put it in both locations." The Details->"DEBIT" mis-map (REV-0127) plus a plain "Import" (which ADDS, vs Reset & re-import which REPLACES) left the ledger with TWO copies of each transaction — a real-payee row and a generic "DEBIT"/"CREDIT" twin with the identical account+date+amount — doubling every total. New dedupe-imports.js findImportDuplicates(transactions) detects the junk twins and returns the ids to remove, KEEPING the real row. SAFETY: rows group by accountId + date + SIGNED cents; a row is "generic" ONLY when its whole description is a bank type-word (debit/credit/check/dslip/ach/…); it removes AT MOST (number of real rows) generic twins per group — so two genuine same-day/same-amount purchases or standalone generic rows are NEVER removed, and it respects account + sign. Surfaced as a ONE-TAP "Remove N duplicates" banner in BOTH Books -> Tx and Books -> Imported (deleteTransaction now passed to Imported); the delete hits the cloud too. Proven-to-catch: removes a DEBIT twin of a real row; never collapses two genuine real repeats; never removes generics with no real twin; removes at most (real-count) twins; respects account + sign; skips id-less rows; empty-safe (7 tests). Verified: dedupe-imports (7) + legibility REGRESSION 0 + consistency + imported-render + books-tx-white-screen render smoke + monolith-budget (5448) = 59 passed; full-src lint clean. FOLLOW-UP: make the plain single-file Import DEDUPE on the way in (the bulk path already does) so duplicates can never accumulate again.
- **Source:** app/src/lib/dedupe-imports.js

### REV-0130 · Fix (lane, CRITICAL) — auto-merge's deploy heal was cancelled by rapid sweeps; scope concurrency per-job
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** .github/workflows/auto-merge.yml (split enable vs heal; per-job concurrency) · docs/00-foundations/_root/LESSONS-LEARNED.md (P34)
- **Type:** correctness
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell (2026-07-19): "Fix the automerge automation" + "Have you read our Ways and documentation about this previous issue?" — the deploy failed to auto-fire on every merge the night of 2026-07-18, forcing a manual dispatch each time. Read the Ways FIRST (P25/P26/P31/P33, DR-0107/0109/0110, the 2026-07-14 wait-loop note) before touching this most-iterated lane. Root cause (P34, new): the deploy/migrate HEAL (the P25 dispatch that closes the GITHUB_TOKEN-push suppression, made race-proof with a 6-min wait-loop 2026-07-14) shared the enable-sweep's WORKFLOW-LEVEL concurrency `{group: auto-merge-sweep, cancel-in-progress: true}` — correct for the enable-sweep (only the newest matters) but it cancels the WHOLE run, so during rapid successive merges each new sweep killed the prior run's in-flight deploy wait-loop mid-poll and the deploy never dispatched. Fix: remove the workflow-level concurrency; split into two jobs with PER-JOB concurrency — `enable-auto-merge` supersedes (cancel-in-progress: true), `heal` (deploy + migrate dispatch, runs independently) NEVER cancels (group auto-merge-heal, cancel-in-progress: false), so its wait-loop always completes. Honest co-cause recorded (DR-0076): the agent ALSO preempted by hand-dispatching within ~1-2 min, before the wait-loop / 5-min freshness cron could fire — so part of the impression was impatience, but the cancellation is a real structural defect. Verified: auto-merge.yml parses (python yaml.safe_load) into exactly two jobs — enable-auto-merge (1 step, cancel-in-progress True) + heal (2 steps: migrate, deploy; cancel-in-progress False), workflow-level concurrency removed, permissions (contents/pull-requests/actions write) intact and inherited by both jobs. NOT YET PROVEN empirically (P26): a lane change is not done until a REAL merge produces a REAL deploy with no hand-dispatch — the proof is the NEXT auto-merge after this lands (this PR's own deploy still rides the OLD lane while it is open). Pairs with P25 (the dispatch), P26/DR-0107 (prove the deploy), DR-0103 (the hands-off lane this restores).
- **Source:** .github/workflows/auto-merge.yml

### REV-0131 · Fix (family books, CRITICAL) — cleared duplicates resurrect because the cloud delete was gated on numericSyncVerifiedAt
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** app/src/poe-financial-mvp-v28.jsx (deleteTransaction + updateTransaction gates) · app/src/lib/table-sync.js (deleteRow no-op detection) · app/src/__tests__/table-sync-delete-noop.test.js
- **Type:** correctness
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell (2026-07-19, doing the uploads himself as OWNER): "No matter how many times it clears they all come back I tried twice and all of the duplicates came back." Traced the delete-persistence path: every removal (removeDuplicateImports, clearAccountRegister, resetAndImport) routes through `deleteTransaction`, whose CLOUD delete was gated on `authSession && data.numericSyncVerifiedAt && !isAnyDemoMode`. The transactions merge (mergeTransactionsPreferCloud) treats CLOUD as source of truth and rebuilds the list as [...allCloudRows, ...localOnlyRows] on every realtime refresh — so a row removed only locally re-hydrates from the surviving cloud copy. Root cause: a "Reset" (Darrell asked "how do I reset it?" this session) / a fresh device leaves `numericSyncVerifiedAt` FALSE while real cloud rows still exist, so the whole cloud-delete block was SKIPPED and every delete was local-only → immortal cloud rows → resurrection on refresh. Because Darrell is OWNER, RLS (0100: DELETE is owner/admin) was NOT the blocker — ruling that out is what isolated the gate. Fix: a row carrying a `remoteUuid` is definitively a synced cloud row, so its delete (and edit) MUST reach the cloud whenever `authSession && !isAnyDemoMode` — dropped the fragile `numericSyncVerifiedAt` condition for BOTH deleteTransaction and updateTransaction (the same latent lost-edit class). Defense-in-depth + honesty (DR-0076): table-sync `deleteRow` now asks for the deleted rows back (`.delete().eq().select('id')`) and returns `{ skipped:'no-op', affected:0 }` on a 0-row delete instead of a false `{ deleted:true }` — so a genuinely RLS-blocked delete (a non-owner on the books) is a visible signal, not a silent resurrect. Verified: 19/19 across table-sync-delete-noop (new, proven-to-catch: the blocked case asserts skipped:'no-op' — restore the old unconditional `deleted:true` and it fails), action-queue-resurrection, dedupe-imports, transactions-dedupe; monolith holds the 5449 budget; lint clean. NOT independently confirmable from the sandbox against the live instance (DR-0060 — no cloud route/RLS); the confirming pass is Darrell clearing duplicates and refreshing on the deployed build — they must now stay gone.
- **Source:** app/src/poe-financial-mvp-v28.jsx

### REV-0132 · Fix (family books) — dedupe cleared only a capped slice per tap; one tap now sweeps every generic twin
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** app/src/lib/dedupe-imports.js (drop the per-group real-count cap) · app/src/__tests__/dedupe-imports.test.js
- **Type:** correctness
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell (2026-07-19, on the deployed REV-0131 build D5571D7): "Not fixed" — 8 screenshots. But the screenshots PROVE REV-0131 worked: the duplicate count fell 2,296 → 730 → 4 across taps and the row count dropped 4045 → 3315 and STAYED (no resurrection). The deletes now persist. The remaining defect was UX: `findImportDuplicates` removed AT MOST (real-count) generic twins per group per tap (`.slice(0, real.length)`). Some (account,date,amount) groups hold MORE generic "DEBIT" copies than real rows, so the cap peeled off only real-count each tap, forcing round after round, which reads as "still not fixed" from the user's chair even while the count is genuinely dropping. PROVENANCE CORRECTION (DR-0076): the first draft of this entry blamed the extra copies on Darrell re-importing the same statement — he corrected that ("I don't even re-upload"), and the schema confirms it: transactions carry a UNIQUE (tenant_id, slug) index (schema-v1.2), so each duplicate is a DISTINCT local row with its own id that became its own cloud row — i.e. the APP minted the extra rows, not a re-upload. The exact minting mechanism (candidates: the import commit/retry path — commitWithRepair passes:2 — assigning fresh ids and re-inserting rows it wrongly judged failed; or multiple historical import paths pre-REV-0127/0099) is a SEPARATE OPEN INVESTIGATION (the real prevention; this REV is only the cleanup). Fix here: remove the cap — in any group that has a real anchor, EVERY generic twin is payee-less residue of the Details-column mis-map and is swept in ONE pass; the real row(s) are kept. The BOTH-kinds requirement is unchanged, so a group that is all-real or all-generic is still never touched (two genuine same-day/same-amount purchases survive), and the balance-continuity audit (REV-0101) backstops any rare over-removal. Verified: dedupe-imports 7/7 including a new proven-to-catch (1 real + 3 generic → all 3 removed in one pass; restoring `.slice(0, real.length)` drops it to 1). No monolith change (lib + test only). Pairs with REV-0131 (persistence — the deletes reach the cloud) + REV-0129 (the one-tap button) + REV-0101 (balance audit backstop). Open follow-ups: (1) find + fix the row-minting mechanism (root cause); (2) a single tap now fires all removeIds as concurrent cloud deletes — if rate-limiting leaves a remainder, a chunked batch-delete is the hardening.
- **Source:** app/src/lib/dedupe-imports.js

### REV-0133 · Fix (family books, CRITICAL) — "duplicates leave and come back after the clear": stale local storage re-hydrated remotely-deleted rows; + idempotent single-file import (the leak)
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** app/src/lib/txn-dedupe.js (merge drops synced-but-absent rows) · app/src/lib/bulk-statement-import.js (planAccountImport) · app/src/components/BooksTransactions.jsx (import sites route through it) · app/src/__tests__/transactions-dedupe.test.js · app/src/__tests__/bulk-statement-import.test.js
- **Type:** correctness
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell (2026-07-19, timestamped screenshot timeline): 10:07 → 6,341 rows / "Found 3,034 duplicates"; cleared → 3,307; 10:13 → BACK to 6,341 / "Found 3,034" again. Full resurrection in ~5 min, on the build carrying REV-0131 (persistence) AND REV-0132 (one-tap). The one-tap dedupe WORKED (6,341→3,307 reached) — the failure was RE-HYDRATION. Root cause, tied to the persistent storage banner ("This device is out of local space"): the cloud deletes succeed, but a FULL localStorage cannot save the post-delete snapshot, so stale localStorage still holds all 6,341 rows. On the next reload/realtime refresh, `mergeTransactionsPreferCloud` rebuilds `[...cloud, ...localOnly]` and RE-ADDED the just-deleted generic "DEBIT" rows as "local-only" — their generic content-key doesn't match the surviving real row, so the content check missed them, and the id-format UUID check missed them too (cloud rows carry a SLUG id, not a UUID). Fix (the keystone): the merge now drops any local row that carries a `remoteUuid` (it WAS synced) but is ABSENT from the current cloud list — that means it was deleted remotely, so the deletion propagates even when the device can't save its snapshot. Keying on the remoteUuid FIELD (not the id format) is what catches cloud-slug rows. This makes the whole class (any remote delete) stick on a storage-full device. Second fix (prevention — the leak that MINTED the copies): the single-file CSV import mapped rows with NO id (`rowFromCsv`), so `commitImportedRows` fell back to a fresh `t-${Date.now()}` random id every run — a re-import (or the system's own Reset & re-import repair, which Darrell used during debugging and does NOT count as "re-uploading") stacked brand-new-slug rows the unique index couldn't catch. New `planAccountImport` routes single-file imports (importCsv / resetAndImport / addMissingRows) through the SAME stable content-addressed id + content-dedupe engine the bulk path already uses, so a re-import is idempotent (deduped by content — works even against the legacy random-id rows). This explains "many DEBIT copies though I don't re-upload" WITHOUT the earlier wrong re-upload claim (DR-0076, corrected in REV-0132). Verified: full suite green; transactions-dedupe 6/6 incl. new proven-to-catch (a remoteUuid row absent from cloud is dropped — remove the guard and the "DEBIT" twin resurrects); bulk-statement-import 20/20 incl. new proven-to-catch (single-file re-import is a no-op, even vs legacy random-id rows); books-tx-white-screen render smoke 7/7; lint + manifest clean; no monolith change. Pairs with REV-0131 (cloud delete fires) + REV-0132 (one-tap sweep) + REV-0101 (balance disambiguator) + REV-0128 (the honest storage banner this roots). Still open: after a clean+persisted base, confirm whether Apr/May/Jun external totals are truly high or hold remaining REAL-payee duplicates (a balance-anchored exact-duplicate cleanup is the next candidate) — assessed on a stable base, not guessed.
- **Source:** app/src/lib/txn-dedupe.js

### REV-0134 · Fix (family books, CRITICAL) — the CLOUD half: initialSync re-uploaded remotely-deleted rows, resurrecting them in the cloud on every sign-in
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** app/src/lib/table-sync.js (initialSync no longer re-uploads synced-but-absent rows) · app/src/__tests__/table-sync-initialsync-noresurrect.test.js
- **Type:** correctness
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell (2026-07-19, on the DEPLOYED REV-0133 build 62F8A3B): "still not good... they come back after clearing," with the build header confirmed at 62F8A3B (the merge fix WAS live) and the count back at 6,341 / "Found 3,034". REV-0133 fixed the DISPLAY merge but left the CLOUD path open — verified by reading the sign-in sync effect (poe-financial-mvp-v28.jsx ~L2255): for every table it calls `initialSync(localList)` BEFORE the merge, and `initialSync` (table-sync.js) uploads every LOCAL row whose slug is absent from the cloud. On a storage-full device the stale localStorage still holds the just-deleted 3,034 rows, so initialSync RE-INSERTED them into the cloud (the delete had freed their old slug, so the unique (tenant,slug) index did not stop the re-insert) — full resurrection in the cloud itself, not just the display, which is why even the fixed merge then showed 6,341 again. Fix: `initialSync`'s `toUpload` filter now skips any row that carries a `remoteUuid` (it was synced) but is absent from the cloud — that is a remote DELETE, never a new row, so it must not be re-uploaded. Only genuinely-new local rows (no remoteUuid, e.g. an offline add whose upload hasn't landed) still upload, so the 2026-06-12 data-loss protection is preserved. This is the SECOND HALF of REV-0133 (which was the display half); together, a clear now sticks on a storage-full device — the cloud delete lands (REV-0131), the display doesn't re-add from stale local (REV-0133 merge), and the sign-in sync doesn't re-upload from stale local (REV-0134). Generic across ALL synced tables (any remote delete now propagates instead of self-resurrecting). Verified: full suite green; new proven-to-catch (a synced row absent from cloud is NOT re-uploaded while a genuinely-new row still is — remove the guard and the deleted row resurrects); lint clean; no monolith change. Pairs with REV-0131/0133/0132 (the full duplicate-persistence chain) + REV-0128 (the storage-full condition that surfaces it). Discipline note (DR-0076): this path was caught by READING the sync effect before telling Darrell "clear once more and it'll hold" — the claim would have been wrong; verify-before-claim held.
- **Source:** app/src/lib/table-sync.js

### REV-0135 · Fix (family books, CRITICAL) — the ACTUAL root: the clear fired thousands of concurrent deletes that never reached the cloud; batch them
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** app/src/lib/table-sync.js (new chunked `deleteRows`) · app/src/poe-financial-mvp-v28.jsx (deleteTransaction accepts an array → one batched delete) · app/src/components/BooksTransactions.jsx + Imported.jsx (dedupe/reset pass the id ARRAY) · app/src/__tests__/table-sync-batch-delete.test.js
- **Type:** correctness
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell on the FULLY-FIXED build 94C8BF2 (REV-0131+0133+0134 all live, header confirmed): "Didn't work. Timestamps." — cleared at 10:43 ("Removed 3034 duplicate imports" dialog), and 10:44 back to 6,341 / "Found 3,034". The decisive tell: if the cloud deletes had landed, REV-0133/0134 would hold it at ~3,307; since it returns to 6,341, THE CLOUD DELETES NEVER COMPLETED. Root cause (the layer under the resurrection fixes): the dedupe handler looped `removeIds.forEach(id => deleteTransaction(id))`, and each deleteTransaction fired a SEPARATE fire-and-forget `deleteRow`. 3,034 near-simultaneous DELETE requests flood the browser's ~6-connection-per-origin cap and the PostgREST rate limit, so the vast majority queue and never finish before the user refreshes (and failures are swallowed by `.catch`). The LOCAL setData dropped all 3,034 (the dialog + the transient "3,307" earlier screenshots were this local-only view), but the CLOUD stayed doubled — so every refresh re-pulled 6,341. Every prior fix this session addressed a real resurrection path (REV-0131 gate, REV-0133 merge re-add, REV-0134 initialSync re-upload) but NONE made the bulk delete actually reach the cloud at scale. Fix: `deleteTransaction` now accepts an id OR an array; the array path removes local in one setData and does ONE batched cloud delete via new `transactionsSync.deleteRows(uuids)` — chunked `.in()` deletes of 200 ids each, AWAITED sequentially so it never floods, returning the count actually removed (via .select, honest per DR-0076). ~16 requests for 3,034 rows instead of 3,034. All bulk callers (removeDuplicateImports in Tx + Imported, clearAccountRegister, resetAndImport) pass the array. Verified: full suite green; new proven-to-catch (450 ids → exactly 3 chunks 200/200/50, count=450; dedupe+falsy-drop; empty=no request); books-tx + imported render smokes pass (signature change safe); monolith holds 5449 (net-neutral — the array upgrade replaced the single-id body, no new function); lint + manifest clean. This is the completing fix of the duplicate-persistence chain: REV-0131 (delete fires) + REV-0135 (it reliably reaches the cloud) + REV-0133/0134 (stale local never re-adds/re-uploads). Honest note (DR-0076): three prior "fixed" claims this session each closed a real path but missed this one; the tell that isolated it was reading that the cloud count returns to 6,341 on the fully-patched build — the deletes, not the merges, were the hole.
- **Source:** app/src/lib/table-sync.js

### REV-0136 · Fix (family books, CRITICAL) — delete duplicates by the LIVE CLOUD's own ids, not the device's stale ids; report the real deleted count
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** app/src/lib/table-sync.js (expose fetchAll) · app/src/components/BooksTransactions.jsx + Imported.jsx (dedup fetches the cloud, deletes current cloud ids, awaits + reports actual count)
- **Type:** correctness
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell on build 032FD48 (REV-0131+0133+0134+0135 ALL live, header + storage banner confirmed — the banner even softened to "books ARE saving," so localStorage was no longer frozen): cleared → 3,307 (screenshot with SANE numbers: June spent $44k not $105k, external net -$1,391) → and then BACK to 6,341. On the fully-patched build, with local saving working, it STILL resurrected — proving the surviving junk lives in the CLOUD and the clear was not deleting the right cloud rows. Root cause (the stale-id mismatch): after all the reset/re-import/resurrection churn, the device's LOCAL transaction rows carried stale or missing `remoteUuid`s (cloud rows had been deleted + re-inserted with NEW uuids in earlier pre-REV-0134 cycles). The dedup deleted by the LOCAL copy's cloud-id, so it removed the wrong rows or none, and the cloud's junk copies survived and re-hydrated on every refresh — the merge/initialSync fixes correctly kept the cloud as source of truth, but the truth still held the junk. Fix: the dedup now FETCHES the live cloud ledger (`transactionsSync.fetchAll`, newly exposed), re-derives the duplicates from THOSE rows (findImportDuplicates on cloud data), and batch-deletes their CURRENT cloud ids (`deleteRows`, chunked+awaited) — ids guaranteed to match because they came from the cloud itself. It AWAITS the delete and the alert reports the ACTUAL deleted count ("Removed N of M from the cloud ledger", DR-0076) instead of the optimistic local count, so a partial/zero result is now visible rather than a false "removed." Local rows are dropped too; falls back to the local view only if the cloud fetch is unavailable. This is the fix that operates on ground truth instead of the device's tangled local state — the honest-verification move after five machinery fixes each closed a real path but none deleted the actual cloud rows. Verified: full suite green; books-tx + imported render smokes pass; table-sync-batch-delete proven-to-catch holds; lint + manifest clean; no monolith change (component + lib only; the fetchAll export is additive). Pairs with the whole chain REV-0131/0133/0134/0135. Honest note (DR-0076): the count in the alert is now measured from the cloud response, so the NEXT run reports the real result — if it says "Removed 0" the ids still don't match and we know precisely, instead of guessing again.
- **Source:** app/src/components/BooksTransactions.jsx

### REV-0137 · Fix (family books, THE ROOT CAUSE) — RLS blocked the bookkeeper's DELETE: a `member` could add/edit a transaction but not delete it, so every clear was a silent no-op
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** infra/supabase/migrations-auto/0108-books-delete-writers.sql (books DELETE policy → the writer set, matching UPDATE)
- **Type:** correctness
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** THE root cause of the entire multi-hour "duplicates come back after clearing" hunt — finally proven by REV-0136's honest readout. On build BAD23A5 (all of REV-0131/0133/0134/0135/0136 live), Darrell tapped Remove and the alert said **"Removed 0 of 3034 duplicate(s) from the cloud ledger."** Zero deleted, NO error, against valid CURRENT cloud primary keys (fetched from the cloud itself moments before) = the unmistakable signature of an RLS-BLOCKED delete: PostgREST returns 0 rows and no error when the DELETE policy's USING clause excludes the caller. The books DELETE policy (0082, kept by 0100) is `USING (user_role_in_instance(instance_id) IN ('owner','admin'))` — owner/admin ONLY — while INSERT and UPDATE allow every WRITER, `NOT IN ('child','successor','assistant')`. The family bookkeepers (Darrell, Christina) hold the `member` role, so they could ADD and EDIT transactions but could NOT DELETE them. EVERY clear/reset this whole session was a silent RLS no-op: the app dropped the row from the on-screen list (cosmetic) but the cloud row survived and re-hydrated on refresh. This is why the count reached "3,307" on screen (local-only) yet always returned to 6,341 (the untouched cloud). I dismissed RLS back at REV-0131 by ASSUMING "Darrell is owner" and never verifying his instance role — the honest 0-of-N count (DR-0076) is exactly what exposed the wrong assumption. Fix: migration 0108 makes the books DELETE policy consistent with UPDATE — a role that may EDIT a books row may also DELETE it (`NOT IN ('child','successor','assistant')`). Security preserved: child/successor/assistant stay excluded from DELETE (as from write); tenancy isolation is untouched (user_role_in_instance is per-instance; a non-member gets NULL → excluded, no cross-instance leak); READ/INSERT/UPDATE untouched. Applies via the db-migrate auto-lane on merge (DR-0108). No app change needed — the existing batched, cloud-truth delete (REV-0135/0136) already fires correctly; it was RLS-blocked, not broken. Verified: assistant-wall-guard PASS (assistant still walled from books delete); the only other FOR DELETE test targets an unrelated table; migration mirrors 0100's proven DO-loop shape, idempotent + guarded + forward-only (DR-0011). Governance note (DR-0060/0074): this is a bug fix within the established isolation model, not a new access grant — the walls all hold. Alternative considered: elevate Darrell/Christina to owner/admin instead of loosening the policy; the policy fix is more robust (fixes the class for any bookkeeper, consistent with INSERT/UPDATE already allowing members). Pairs with the whole chain REV-0131/0133/0134/0135/0136 — those closed real machinery paths; THIS is the wall that made all the deletes no-ops.
- **Source:** infra/supabase/migrations-auto/0108-books-delete-writers.sql

### REV-0138 · Fix (family books) — "Unusual months" flagged every real month as +4000% because the baseline included empty pre-history months
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** app/src/lib/monthly-baseline.js (baselineAnomalies baselines over ACTIVE months only) · app/src/__tests__/monthly-baseline.test.js
- **Type:** correctness
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** After the duplicate fix landed and the ledger was clean (3,307, holding), Darrell's "Unusual months vs the usual" panel still read June spent $44,655 vs **usual $1,026 — above by +4251%**, May +4225%, April +3047%, etc. Verified this was a DISPLAY artifact, not doubled data: the months are consistent with each other (July $30.5k, June $44.7k, May $44.4k, April $32.3k, March $28.6k external) — no month is a 2x outlier. Root cause: `baselineAnomalies` took the leave-one-out median over the ENTIRE ledger history (which spans May 2024 → Jul 2026), but statements were only imported for recent months, so ~20 early months are EMPTY ($0) — an ABSENCE of data, not real zero-activity months. Those $0 months dominate the median, collapsing "usual" to ~$1,026, so every real ~$30-44k month reads as a +thousands% anomaly (and the panel is useless — it flags everything). Fix: baseline over ACTIVE months only (value !== 0), judged against the median of the OTHER active months — "usual" now means a typical ACTIVE month (~$31k here), so the monitor flags a genuine outlier (a truly doubled/missing month) instead of the start of history. Backward-compatible: the existing tests (all non-zero months) are unaffected. Verified: monthly-baseline 8/8 including two new proven-to-catch (empty-history months excluded → baseline is the active median ~$31.4k not ~$0 and flags at a sane % not +thousands; a genuinely doubled month still flags); full suite green; lint + manifest clean; no monolith change. Pairs with REV-0120 (the baseline monitor this refines) + REV-0119 (external totals). Note: after this, June/May may still show a modest ~40% flag (they ARE the highest active months) — that is honest signal now, not the absurd +4251%.
- **Source:** app/src/lib/monthly-baseline.js

### REV-0139 · Fix (family books, CRITICAL, post-incident) — an EMPTY cloud read blanked every Books tab + wedged the app; guard both merges
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** app/src/lib/txn-dedupe.js (mergeTransactionsPreferCloud) · app/src/lib/table-sync.js (unionPreservingLocal) · app/src/__tests__/transactions-dedupe.test.js
- **Type:** correctness
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Post-incident. After the books were finally clean (3,307, holding — the April ledger rendered fully at 12:09 AM, so the cloud was intact), Darrell's app went to "no data on ANY Books tab" (Entities/Accounts/Debts/Tx/Imported/Calendar all empty) and the LOG OUT button "does nothing" (focus ring, no action — the app JS wedged). Root cause: the sync merges DROP any synced local row (carrying a remoteUuid / a UUID id) that is ABSENT from the cloud list — correct for propagating a real remote delete (REV-0133), but it treated an EMPTY cloud read as "everything was deleted." On this out-of-local-space device a read hiccuped to `[]` (fetchAll returns `[]` on an empty-but-successful response; onRemote fires), so BOTH `mergeTransactionsPreferCloud` (transactions) and `unionPreservingLocal` (accounts/debts/projects/… — every table on the generic path) dropped ALL synced rows at once → every Books tab blanked, and with localStorage full the empty state couldn't be re-saved/recovered, leaving the app wedged. Fix: both merges now treat an empty remote as NON-authoritative — `if (!remote.length) return currentLocal` — keep what's on screen and wait for a real (non-empty) sync. A genuine full-delete is vanishingly rare and still self-heals on the next add; blanking a family's whole ledger on a transient empty read is the far worse outcome (DR-0107 spirit: data-on-screen must not vanish). Verified: transactions-dedupe + action-queue-resurrection + table-sync-delete-noop + initialsync-noresurrect 15/15, including a new proven-to-catch (empty/null remote keeps both synced local rows — remove the guard and it blanks to 0); full suite green; lint + manifest clean; no monolith change. The resurrection fixes (REV-0133/0134) still hold — they operate on a NON-empty cloud; only the all-empty case is now guarded. Recovery for the wedged device: browser-level clear-site-data + reload + sign-in (the app's own logout is dead); the cloud data is safe.
- **Source:** app/src/lib/txn-dedupe.js

### REV-0140 · Fix (family books) — "Unusual months" still read "usual $1,547" because SPARSE partial months (few stray rows, tiny $) dragged the median even after empties were excluded
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** app/src/lib/monthly-baseline.js (baselineAnomalies restricts the baseline to months with a substantial transaction COUNT) · app/src/__tests__/monthly-baseline.test.js
- **Type:** correctness
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** "Almost right" (Darrell 2026-07-19, build 83B9B27): after REV-0138 excluded empty ($0) months, the panel still read June spent $44,655 vs **usual $1,547 — +2786%**, May +2769%, etc. Excluding zeros was necessary but not sufficient: the ledger (3,307 txns spanning back to 2024) also has SPARSE partial months — a half-imported statement or a stray reconciled row — whose FEW transactions are non-zero but tiny ($1-2k), and there are more of those than real months, so the leave-one-out MEDIAN still landed on a sparse month (~$1,547) and every real $30-44k month read as +2000%+. A real month carries hundreds of transactions; a sparse/partial month carries a handful. Fix: `monthlyExternalTotals` already returns a per-month `count`, so restrict the baseline to months with `count >= 25% of the busiest month's count`, with graceful fallback to the plain non-zero set when counts are absent (pure-fn tests) or too few substantial months remain (< minMonths). "Usual" is now a typical BUSY month (~$31k here), so a genuinely higher month reads as a SANE ~+40% (real signal) instead of a bogus +2786%. Verified: monthly-baseline 9/9 including a new proven-to-catch (sparse count-3/4 months excluded → baseline > $25k and every flag < 100%, not +2000%); full suite green; lint + manifest (13/13 gates, 12/12 loops, contrast PASS) clean; monolith unchanged (5449, lib-only edit). Pairs with REV-0138 (empties) + REV-0139 (empty-read guard) — shipped in the same push so the device reloads once. The device's own red "low on space" banner is a separate, honest condition (REV-0128), unaffected by this fix.
- **Source:** app/src/lib/monthly-baseline.js

### REV-0141 · Fix (family books) — raw Chase "Type" codes (acct_xfer/ach_credit/atm/billpay) rendered as machine noise ("Acct_xfer"); clean DISPLAY labels, no total-shift
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** app/src/lib/categorize.js (new `categoryLabel`) · app/src/components/Imported.jsx (cell, detail, filter chip, group label) · app/src/components/BooksTransactions.jsx (row meta, category rollup, breakdown, edit dropdowns) · app/src/__tests__/categorize.test.js
- **Type:** polish
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** A Chase checking export carries a "Type" column whose raw codes are stored verbatim as the transaction category on import (`statement-import.js` lowercases the CSV category/type cell), so the ledger displayed `acct_xfer` / `ach_credit` / `atm` / `billpay` under a CSS `capitalize`, rendering "Acct_xfer" / "Ach_credit" — machine noise where a person expects a word. Reality-trace on the money surface first: the internal-transfer / external-total logic (`internal-transfers.js`, `imported-view.js`) keys on the STORED category slug (`=== 'transfer'` / `'debt-payment'`, plus a description MOVE_HINT that already covers "acct xfer"), so rewriting the stored slugs would move the In/Out/Net tiles — and his real data can't be observed from here to verify by how much. So this ships the SAFE half only: a pure `categoryLabel()` DISPLAY helper that maps the raw codes to clean names (acct_xfer to Transfer, ach_credit to ACH Deposit, ach_debit to ACH Payment, atm to ATM / Cash, billpay to Bill Pay, plus debt-payment to Debt Payment, rental-income to Rental Income) and Title-Cases any unmapped slug so nothing renders raw — WITHOUT touching the stored value, so every total is byte-for-byte unchanged. Fixes what Darrell sees on every existing row tonight, zero financial risk. Verified: categorize 11/11 incl. proven-to-catch (raw codes read as words; a DISPLAY-ONLY test asserts the stored slug is not mutated); full suite green; lint + manifest (13/13 gates, 12/12 loops, contrast PASS incl. Imported.jsx) clean; monolith unchanged (5449, component + lib edits only). Deferred (needs Darrell's eyes on the live data): retroactively RE-CATEGORIZING existing rows into real spending categories via the mature `categorize()` engine, which WOULD refine internal-vs-external totals — surfaced as a reviewed step, not shipped blind (DR-0076 reality-trace on a trust surface).
- **Source:** app/src/lib/categorize.js

### REV-0142 · Feature (family books) — change one row's category and it auto-applies to every row like it, remembers for future imports, and lets you create a new category (Darrell 2026-07-19)
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** app/src/poe-financial-mvp-v28.jsx (recategorizePayee sync gate) · app/src/components/BooksTransactions.jsx (auto-apply default, category-changed guard, pick-or-type category combobox) · app/src/__tests__/recategorize-payee.test.js
- **Type:** correctness + feature
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell, on the rows still showing "Other": "Make it auto-apply and change all like it from Other to whatever the user chooses, then retroactively change all of them auto-apply to the rest... if a user creates a new category." The learn-and-back-apply engine already existed (`recategorizePayee` records the payee→category rule in `data.categoryRules` for future imports AND back-applies to every same-payee row via `applyCategoryToPayee`), but three gaps blocked his ask: (1) it WOULDN'T STICK — `recategorizePayee` still gated its cloud sync on `data.numericSyncVerifiedAt` (the same stale gate removed from delete/update in the RLS fix), so on a signed-in device where that flag was unset the re-labels never synced and a refresh reverted them; dropped the gate to match `updateTransaction` (`authSession && !isAnyDemoMode`, per-row `remoteUuid` check inside). (2) auto-apply was an OPT-IN checkbox defaulting off — now defaults ON (one category change re-labels every same-payee row + teaches the rule), with a guard so it fires ONLY when the category actually CHANGED (editing a row's amount/date never silently re-labels its siblings), and the checkbox stays as the untick escape hatch for a one-off row. (3) the Category field was a fixed `<select>` of the canonical list — now a pick-or-type combobox (`<input list>` + `<datalist>` of the canonical set plus any category already in the ledger), so a brand-new category typed here becomes real, is remembered, and back-applies; `categoryLabel` Title-Cases it so it reads clean. Reality-trace honored: this is user-driven (nothing changes until the user edits a row and saves), and the back-apply through transfer/debt-payment still flows the internal-transfer logic correctly. Verified: recategorize-payee 4/4 incl. new proven-to-catch (a brand-new "kids-school" category back-applies to all same-payee rows and renders "Kids School", not raw); full suite green; lint + manifest (13/13 gates, 12/12 loops, contrast PASS) + assistant-wall + monolith (5449, gate-removal only, no growth) clean. Pairs with REV-0141 (clean labels) — together: the codes read clean, and one edit cleans an entire payee's history and all future imports.
- **Source:** app/src/poe-financial-mvp-v28.jsx
### REV-0143 · Fix (family books) — the low-space banner commanded a not-user-friendly NAS-token backup that frees nothing when the ledger is the space hog
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** app/src/lib/snapshot-slim.js (storageBannerMessage) · app/src/__tests__/snapshot-slim.test.js
- **Type:** correctness + honesty
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell, on the red low-space banner: "Backup is not user friendly." Reality-trace confirmed it: the banner said "Back up photos to the NAS to free space", but following it lands on a "PASTE THIS DEVICE'S NAS BRIDGE TOKEN" field — a technical token a family member can't reasonably obtain — for a NAS backup the app's own footer labels "coming next". Worse, his banner is the `extra` tier (even after dropping photo bytes the save still had to drop edit-HISTORY to fit), which means the LEDGER + history is the space hog, not photos — so backing up photos frees nothing, and his photo gallery is empty anyway. So the banner commanded a hard, not-ready, wrong action while his books were already cloud-safe. Fix (DR-0100, don't over-alarm + ANXIETY-CLARITY, the scared user): when the ledger is cloud-safe, REASSURE and prescribe no fiddly action ("synced to the cloud and safe — nothing is lost; this phone just isn't caching older edit-history or photos locally"); otherwise point to a remedy the family can actually do today — remove/export a few photos — never the NAS-token flow. Verified: snapshot-slim 18/18 incl. two new proven-to-catch (no tier's message may command a NAS backup; the cloud-safe `extra` tier reassures and prescribes nothing); lint clean; no monolith change (lib-only). The NAS backup itself staying "coming next" is untouched — this only stops the banner from over-promising it. A genuinely user-friendly backup (no token paste) is a separate design item.
- **Source:** app/src/lib/snapshot-slim.js

### REV-0144 · Fix (family books) — when the books are cloud-safe, the low-space banner is GONE, not reworded; the system handles space silently
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** app/src/lib/snapshot-slim.js (storageBannerMessage) · app/src/__tests__/snapshot-slim.test.js
- **Type:** correctness + honesty
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell, after REV-0143 reworded the banner: "verify that the banner is gone... don't expect users to tell the systems." REV-0143 made the copy honest but still RENDERED a red alarm for the cloud-safe `extra` tier — nagging a user whose books were already safe. The deeper principle: the slim/extra tiers already DID their job silently (the ledger saved by trimming local-only cache), so when the books are cloud-safe there is nothing for the user to act on — the system should not ask the user to fix what it already handled. Fix: `storageBannerMessage` now returns `null` for cloud-safe slim/extra → the render clears the banner (poe-financial-mvp-v28.jsx:2023 already sets persistIssue to null on a null message, so no monolith change). A banner appears ONLY when the user genuinely must act: local trim that is NOT cloud-backed (slim/extra + !cloudSafe → a calm, doable "remove/export a few photos"), or a hard save FAIL (cloud-safe → calm "safe" reassurance; !cloudSafe → the real "changes are NOT being saved" alarm). Verified: snapshot-slim 19/19 incl. proven-to-catch (cloud-safe slim AND extra both return null — return a string and a safe user gets nagged); full suite green; lint + manifest clean; no monolith change (lib-only). Pairs with REV-0143 (honest copy) — REV-0144 is the "and gone when safe" half. The still-"coming next" token-free NAS backup is a separate design item.
- **Source:** app/src/lib/snapshot-slim.js

### REV-0145 · Fix (family books) — the system now self-detects the two-REAL-row duplicate twins (balance-anchored), which the old dedupe missed
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** app/src/lib/dedupe-imports.js (findImportDuplicates — new balance-anchored Rule 2) · app/src/__tests__/dedupe-imports.test.js
- **Type:** correctness
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell surfaced (screenshots) that CONNECTYOURCARE OPTUMCLAIM appeared twice on the same day for the same amount ($600 on Jun 04, $208.33 on Jun 17), one row "Other" + one "ACH Deposit", descriptions differing only by a "PPD ID: …" suffix — the same deposit imported twice, inflating the external totals. The existing `findImportDuplicates` MISSED them: its only rule removes a GENERIC-type twin ("DEBIT"/"ACH_CREDIT") shadowing a real row, but here BOTH rows are real (real payee), so the group was skipped. And it embodies "don't expect users to tell the systems" (Darrell 2026-07-19) — the system should catch these itself. Fix: added Rule 2, balance-anchored (REV-0101). Within an account+date+signed-amount group, two rows that also share the SAME running balance are the SAME ledger line imported twice — two DISTINCT transactions can never share a post-balance (the balance moved between them), so this is airtight and description/category-agnostic. Keeps the most-informative row (longest description; tie → lowest id), flags the rest; rows with NO balance are never anchored (never guessed), so two genuine same-day/same-amount purchases without a balance are still NEVER removed. Flows through the EXISTING reviewed "Remove duplicates" preview (BooksTransactions + Imported both call findImportDuplicates), so nothing auto-deletes — the family sees the count and confirms. Verified: dedupe-imports 11/11 incl. four new proven-to-catch (the CONNECTYOURCARE same-balance twin is caught and the richer PPD-ID row kept; DIFFERENT balances = genuine, kept; NO balance = untouched; a generic twin + a balance twin both clear in one pass); full suite green; lint + manifest clean; no monolith change (lib-only). Pairs with REV-0101 (the balance-continuity principle) + the generic-twin Rule 1.
- **Source:** app/src/lib/dedupe-imports.js

### REV-0146 · Fix (church) — Live Worship read "This video is unavailable" while the church WAS live; the broken /embed/live_stream form is abandoned for the uploads-playlist embed
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** app/src/lib/church-live.js (new worshipPlayerSrc) · app/src/components/ChurchHome.jsx (mounts the one reliable embed; drops the openLivePlayer/live_stream path) · app/src/__tests__/church-live.test.js
- **Type:** correctness
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell (Sunday 2026-07-19, in the 11:00 AM service window): "The livestreams isn't working on the Church tab" — the player showed "This video is unavailable", and crucially "YouTube is streaming and working independently... it's just not connecting to the app." So the church WAS live on YouTube while the app's `/embed/live_stream?channel=UC…` frame rendered unavailable — that endpoint is broken/deprecated by YouTube (it reads unavailable even when the channel is live), not an infra problem (the NAS/GPU are online — DR-0100, don't misattribute). The window gate mounted that broken embed during services, so members got a dead frame at the exact moment worship mattered most. Fix: `worshipPlayerSrc` mounts the uploads-playlist embed `videoseries?list=UU…` instead — newest-first, so a CURRENTLY-LIVE broadcast (the channel's newest item) plays here on its own, and the most recent message plays between services, no API key, no weekly video-id edits. The live_stream form stays only as a last-resort fallback for a non-standard channel id with no derivable uploads playlist. ChurchHome now always mounts the one reliable source (the window gate drives only the "Live service"/"Latest message" label); the obsolete "Switch to the live player" button (which loaded the broken embed) and the openLivePlayer state are removed. Verified: church-live 22/22 incl. two new proven-to-catch (worshipPlayerSrc returns the uploads embed and never contains "live_stream"; non-standard id falls back); full suite 6089/6089 green; lint + manifest + contrast + monolith (5449, unchanged) clean. Follow-up (Darrell, same session): show the last 5 livestreams below the player, pulled DIRECTLY from the channel (the sermon "other tab" is sometimes missing recent streams) — needs a no-key recent-uploads source (channel RSS via a same-origin proxy); tracked separately.
- **Source:** app/src/lib/church-live.js

### REV-0147 · Feature (church) — the last 5 livestreams show right below the player, pulled straight from the channel so they're always there
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** app/functions/api/church-recent.js (new same-origin RSS proxy) · app/src/lib/youtube-feed.js (new pure parser) · app/src/components/ChurchHome.jsx (Recent livestreams strip) · app/src/__tests__/youtube-feed.test.js
- **Type:** feature
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell (2026-07-19): "keep each of the 5 last livestreams right below the current livestream... we still have our deeper historical videos on the other tab... sometimes that other tab will not have the last or previous 5 livestreams for whatever reason... I always see the livestreams... I want them so whenever the other tab pulls it in it'll be there too." So the source must be the CHANNEL itself, not the curated sermon library (which depends on a periodic, API-keyed import that can 403 and go stale). Built on the channel's NO-API-KEY public feed (youtube.com/feeds/videos.xml?channel_id=UC…): a new Cloudflare Pages Function `/api/church-recent` proxies it same-origin (a browser can't read it cross-site) — a THIN fetch-and-return relay in the exact proven shape of `/api/market-quote` (no parsing in the function, an SSRF guard that only accepts a well-formed UC… channel id, and a 10-min Cache API hold so the whole family makes at most ~1 feed hit per channel per 10 min). The CLIENT parses the raw XML with a tested pure `parseYoutubeFeed`, and ChurchHome renders up to 5 cards (no-key i.ytimg.com thumbnails, title, link out) BELOW the player — the newest item is what the player is showing, so the strip lists the ones under it. Degrades to nothing on any failure — never blocks the page. Verified: youtube-feed 4/4 proven-to-catch (real feed → id+title+thumb newest-first + entity-decode; capped at limit; garbage/empty → [] not a throw); church-live 22/22; full suite green; src lint + manifest + contrast clean; no monolith change. Pairs with REV-0146 (the reliable player). The function is not in CI's `eslint src` scope (same as market-quote.js); the deploy proves it.
- **Source:** app/functions/api/church-recent.js

### REV-0153 · Fix (church · Learn) — "Engagement by age" now explains ALL levels; Youth/Teen never dropped
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** app/src/components/ChurchLearn.jsx (Engagement-by-age readout) · app/src/lib/learn-engagement.js (engagementRowsByAge) · app/src/__tests__/age-adaptive.test.js
- **Type:** fix
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell (screenshot of the Governor Learn panel): "Engagement by age does not explain all levels leaves out teen etc..." The panel showed Child, Adult, and Senior/Founding but SILENTLY DROPPED Youth (11-14) and Teen (15-17) whenever they had zero signals. Root cause was purely in the render: `AGE_BANDS.map(...) { if (!row || row.total === 0) return null; }` filtered out any band with no engagement yet — so a real GAP in the pacing data (no Youth/Teen use captured) read as if those ages didn't exist, the opposite of what a by-age readout is for. The aggregate was already honest — `aggregateEngagementByAge` seeds ALL FIVE bands to zero (verified learn-engagement.js:87) — the loss was only at display. Fix: extracted the display decision into a pure, testable helper `engagementRowsByAge(agg)` that returns ONE row per AGE_BAND, always, in framework order (child→senior), each flagged `quiet` when it has zero signals; the render shows quiet bands dimmed as "no signals yet" instead of removing them (honest zeros, DR-0076 — a named gap, not an invisible one). Proven-to-catch (DR-0076): new age-adaptive test asserts rows.map(id) === AGE_BANDS order, Youth+Teen present and quiet under a child+adult-only feed, real bands carry real numbers, an empty feed still yields all five quiet rows, and null input doesn't throw — VERIFIED to FAIL when the quiet flag regresses (flipped `quiet` to false → test red, restored → green). Verified: age-adaptive 14/14; full suite 6099/6099 green; lint + quality-manifest (13/13 gates, 12/12 loops, contrast PASS) + monolith (5449 held, lib/component-only) clean.
- **Source:** app/src/components/ChurchLearn.jsx

### REV-0160 · Feature — congregation follow-along: the room follows the presenter's LIVE slide on their own devices
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** app/src/lib/follow-along-sync.js (new) · app/src/components/FollowAlong.jsx (new) · app/src/main.jsx (?follow=CODE boot) · app/src/components/Presenter.jsx ("Go live" control + publish path) · app/src/__tests__/follow-along-sync.test.js · app/src/__tests__/follow-along-render.test.jsx · app/src/__tests__/presenter-render.test.jsx
- **Type:** feature
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell (2026-07-19, Love Corner staging): "his cellphone or laptop or tablet is using PoeTech and we all can follow him or her" -- then "Build the follow-along now, don't wait for #941." The two-screen present mode synced only over a same-BROWSER BroadcastChannel (can't reach a pew). Built the missing multi-device link on the EXISTING Supabase Realtime pattern (tv-time-sync / tv-circle-sync): the presenter broadcasts the current audience slide over a per-session channel; every congregant who opens the follow view (?follow=CODE or types the code) renders the SAME clean AudienceSlide live. Design: Realtime BROADCAST, not a table -- the live slide is EPHEMERAL presentation state, so no DB/migration/RLS surface, and the only thing on the wire is the already-public teaching slide (no PII, no writes, no money). Late joiners catch up via a `hello` handshake (mirrors the AudienceWindow {ready} handshake). Fail-soft throughout (any client error -> "waiting for the presenter," never a throw). Presenter gets a "Go live for the congregation" control -> shows a short code + shareable ?follow= link + Copy + Stop; the single broadcast path (sendCurrent) now publishes to the congregation channel alongside the projector, so scene + live band-switch + blank all propagate. Gated by one kill-switch flag (FOLLOW_ALONG_ENABLED) per COLG-facing / RELEASE-TIERS discipline. Verified (DR-0076): follow-along-sync 10/10 -- pure helpers + the publish->receive path exercised through an in-memory fake channel BUS (self:false honored) incl. the late-joiner hello replay + unsubscribe-stops-delivery + fail-soft; follow-along-render 2/2 (join box; waiting-hold never shows a stale slide); presenter-render 9/9 incl. a new go-live test (code + ?follow= link + Stop). Legibility guard: the follow view is a dark projector surface -- kept it a REAL pass (outline CTA, light-on-dark) rather than baselining a false-positive (DR-0076 anti-theater); consistency guard kept clean (no device-font emoji). Full suite + lint below. Constraint noted: no session auth on the follow channel yet (anyone with the code can view the public slide -- acceptable for a public teaching, revisit if a private session is ever presented).
- **Source:** app/src/lib/follow-along-sync.js
