### REV-0155 · Presenter/LED wall — slides TOP-align so the speaker (below the words) never blocks the text
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** app/src/components/AudienceWindow.jsx · app/src/components/Presenter.jsx (present-on-this-screen + the class-screen preview) · app/src/components/FollowAlong.jsx
- **Type:** ui-ux
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell (2026-07-19, screenshot of the live class screen): "Led Wall slides should default to the top of the wall or presenter so the speakers can have a black background and they may not block the words; however if they do it will only be the bottom of the Led Wall" -- and "Speakers will always be below the words." The audience slide was vertically CENTERED, so on a tall LED wall the words landed at head/torso height where the speaker standing in front blocks them. Fix: every full-screen audience surface now TOP-aligns the slide (justifyContent center -> flex-start) -- AudienceWindow (the ?audience=1 projector/LED-wall view), the presenter's "Present on this screen" cast view, the in-presenter class-screen PREVIEW (so it mirrors the wall), and the FollowAlong live view (so a follow view cast onto a wall matches). Words sit at the top with the padding already in place; the black background fills the bottom, so the speaker (below the words) only ever covers empty black. The small placeholders stay centered (the waiting/ended hold keeps margin:auto; the follow join box keeps justifyContent:center) -- only real slides top-align. CSS-only, no logic/schema change. Verified: presenter-render 9/9, follow-along-render 2/2, consistency 13/13, legibility 18/18 (dark projector palette unchanged) green; lint clean; full suite below. Pairs with REV-0152 (concise slides) -- shorter slides + top-align together keep the words clear of the speaker.
- **Source:** app/src/components/AudienceWindow.jsx

### REV-0156 · Presenter — points render as separated cards (stand-alone + together); each lesson opens with a TITLE card
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** app/src/components/AudienceSlide.jsx (point cards) · app/src/lib/presentable.js (leading title scene; title untimed) · app/src/__tests__/presentable.test.js
- **Type:** ui-ux
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Two Darrell notes (2026-07-19, screenshots). (1) "These points need to be separate out more so they stand alone and together visually": the bullet points were a plain tight list. Now each point is its OWN visual block -- a tinted card (rgba white 0.05) with a sage (#C9D9A6) left accent bar + an accent marker (a filled dot, or the number when ordered) + generous column gap -- so each point STANDS ALONE, while the uniform card style + aligned column keeps them reading TOGETHER as one set. Kept <li> semantics (list stays a real list); light-on-dark, no new legibility/consistency debt. (2) "The initial slide should be the title of the Lesson so we can have that as a background until the speakers begin": lessonPresentable now leads with a TITLE CARD (Part 1) -- the lesson title + today's anchor reference -- a standing background on the wall as the room gathers, until the speaker advances. It is untimed (excluded from targetMin so the teaching length is unchanged) and rides the reflow as a 1-min supplementary (first dropped when time is tight). Verified: presentable 74/74 (title-first structure, targetMin unchanged at 40, fallback = title + content), presenter-render 9/9, book-corpus 13/13, consistency 13/13, legibility 18/18; lint clean; full suite below. Pairs with REV-0152 (concise slides) + REV-0155 (top-align). Follow-up in flight (Darrell, same session): show each CITED scripture's verbatim text on its slide so the audience reads the Word directly -- next change.
- **Source:** app/src/components/AudienceSlide.jsx

### REV-0157 · Presenter — each CITED Scripture shown verbatim on its slide (the room reads the Word directly)
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** app/src/lib/presentable.js (scriptureRefsInText; citedRefs on teaching beats; buildSlideForScene passthrough) · app/src/components/AudienceSlide.jsx (async resolve + render) · app/src/__tests__/presentable.test.js · app/src/__tests__/audience-cited-render.test.jsx
- **Type:** feature
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell (2026-07-19): "each scripture should be shown so it can be seen in context when cited on the page... for the audience to see and eat the Word directly." Before, only the OPENER showed verbatim Scripture (the anchor); the teaching beats cited many references but the room saw only the reference. Now the builder extracts the Scripture references a teaching beat cites (new pure scriptureRefsInText: matches Book Ch:V incl. a leading 1-3, "of" for Song of Solomon, and -ranges; deduped + capped), attaches them as audience.citedRefs, and AudienceSlide resolves each to VERBATIM KJV from the sovereign in-app corpus (bible-kjv.js verseText, async) and renders them in a set-apart panel (sage left-rule, reference labelled + verse italic) BELOW the concise points -- so the main idea stays prominent while the room reads the actual Word in context. Fail-soft + no-fabrication (DR-0076): a reference that doesn't resolve is silently dropped, never invented; over-matches from the regex fall away at resolve time. Verified: presentable 73/73 incl. new proven-to-catch (scriptureRefsInText dedup/cap/multi-word; a beat carries its citedRefs to the built slide); audience-cited-render 2/2 -- the REAL async resolve->render path through an injected fake corpus (a cited "Jude 3" renders verbatim; an unresolvable "Nowhere 9:9" is dropped); consistency 13/13 + legibility 18/18 (light-on-dark panel, no new debt); full suite exit 0, 0 failures; lint clean. Follow-up in flight (Darrell, same session): a running side-rail listing the Scriptures cited SO FAR up to the current slide, with a total -- next change.
- **Source:** app/src/lib/presentable.js

### REV-0158 · Presenter — a running Scripture side-rail: every reference cited so far, with the lesson total
- **Date:** 2026-07-19
- **Surface:** app/src/lib/presentable.js (buildSlideForScene computes scripturesSoFar + scripturesTotal) · app/src/components/AudienceSlide.jsx (two-column layout + the rail) · app/src/__tests__/presentable.test.js · app/src/__tests__/audience-cited-render.test.jsx
- **Type:** feature
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell (2026-07-19): "on the sides have [a] cited list of the scriptures ... that are being used up until the current slide as a running list ... until the end with a total ... all in its own space to the side looking nice." buildSlideForScene now computes, for the current index, the CUMULATIVE list of every Scripture reference put before the room from slide 0 through this one (each scene's anchor split on ';' + its citedRefs, deduped in order) as `scripturesSoFar`, plus the lesson's grand total `scripturesTotal`. AudienceSlide renders in two columns: the slide content (left) and a Scripture RAIL (right, its own bordered space) -- a header "Scriptures · N of TOTAL", the running list of references (monospace), the CURRENT slide's references glowing sage (#C9D9A6) and the earlier trail in #CFC9BD, capped to the most recent 16 with a "+N earlier" when the list is long (a wall-overflow guard). It composes with the top-align (rail is to the SIDE, away from the center/below speaker) and the per-slide verbatim panel (REV-0157). Verified: presentable 74/74 incl. new proven-to-catch (scripturesSoFar is cumulative + deduped + grows; scripturesTotal >= running count); audience-cited-render 3/3 incl. the rail render ("Scriptures · 3 of 8", the trail present); consistency 13/13 + legibility 18/18 (rail is light-on-dark, verified tokens, no new debt); full suite exit 0, 0 failures; lint + manifest clean.
- **Source:** app/src/components/AudienceSlide.jsx

### REV-0174 · Imported — inspect duplicates before merging (inline candidate view + a different-dates guard)
- **Date:** 2026-07-20
- **Surface:** app/src/components/Imported.jsx (combineSelected date guard + learning gate; inspectDupSig state; groupMembers; learned-group Inspect disclosure) · app/src/__tests__/imported-render.test.jsx
- **Type:** feature
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell (2026-07-20, two messages + a Salary screenshot): "I'll need to inspect the duplicates... maybe a dropdown menu... date time same exactly or differences... need to be able to inspect the dates of the duplicates" and "there should be two entries per month for this salary." Real-world truth: University of IL payroll posts TWICE a month (Jul 01 $2,271.97 + Jul 15 $2,274.78) -- same payee, different date/amount, NOT a duplicate. Reality-trace: learned-duplicate groups are keyed by payeeKey|date|amount|account, so two-per-month can never enter a learned group; the gap was the manual multi-select Combine, which warned only on amount, not date. Two moves: (1) each learned group gets an Inspect toggle that expands IN PLACE (DR-0201, no jumping) to show every candidate row's date . account . full description . amount, plus a plain note on whether descriptions match exactly (bank rows carry a date, not a clock time -- shown honestly, no fabricated timestamp, DR-0076); (2) manual Combine now warns distinctly when selected rows span DIFFERENT dates ("separate payments -- like a salary that posts twice a month -- not duplicates") and lists the dates, and a combine only TEACHES the payee when rows are same-amount AND same-date so a different-date merge never trains a false shape. Verified: imported-render 13/13 incl. +2 proven-to-catch (a different-dates combine warns with both dates listed and does not merge when declined; a learned group's Inspect expands aria-expanded false->true and reveals date + account + the same-date/amount confirmation); consistency 13/13 (rem fonts, geometric glyphs -- no new emoji/fixed-px debt); contrast PASS; full suite 6231/6231, exit 0. DR-0202; memory feedback_inspect_duplicates_before_merge.
- **Source:** app/src/components/Imported.jsx

### REV-0175 · Imported — one-sweep "Clean up duplicates" (AI clears exact copies up front, keep-exceptions by uncheck)
- **Date:** 2026-07-20
- **Surface:** app/src/lib/learned-dedupe.js (findExactDuplicates) · app/src/components/Imported.jsx (bulk panel + per-row Inspect; consolidated the learned-dup list into it) · app/src/__tests__/learned-dedupe.test.js · app/src/__tests__/imported-render.test.jsx
- **Type:** feature
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell (2026-07-20, several messages + screenshots incl. the deployed 17-group "Duplicates the system learned from you" panel): "everyone has been a duplicate, can we clean this up ... they can add back what is not there ... AI cleans up these obvious duplicates that are exactly the same date and amount ... we don't pay twice for the exact same thing, almost ever"; and "it already works great, however initially this should be cleaned up especially since I'm just agreeing because it's right." The per-group Inspect/Combine (DR-0202) is correct but makes the family rubber-stamp Combine group after group; "initially" (first import) the learned-only panel is empty exactly when dups are worst. New pure findExactDuplicates groups the WHOLE ledger by dedupeSignature (payee+date+amount+account), no teaching gate, keeps the fullest row, returns the extra copies -- a superset of the taught-only suggestions. One "Clean up duplicates" panel lists every group pre-checked to remove with a total count; one Remove-N button clears them all; the rare real repeat (two $200 ATM withdrawals -- the "almost ever") is a single UNCHECK that keeps both, the "add back" done safely up front before any deletion (post-deletion undo deferred, re-review 2026-10-20). The separate learned-dup list is folded IN: per-row Inspect (inline date . account . full description, DR-0201) carried over, so ONE surface bulk-removes / keeps / inspects -- less clutter. The sweep teaches each removed payee so future identical imports are recognized. Reality-trace: real dup imports carry IDENTICAL descriptions (strict full-payee signature matches them; different payees never collapse); different-DATE paychecks can never share a signature (salary safe by construction). Verified: learned-dedupe +2 (groups every exact repeat + keeps fullest + ignores solo; never groups two different-date paychecks); imported-render +1 and the DR-0202 inspect test re-pointed here (bulk panel renders, unchecking a real ATM keeps it and drops the Remove count, Remove targets only still-checked copies, Inspect expands inline); contrast fix (moved a ternary color to classes so the guard resolves it); lint + consistency + contrast + legibility green; full suite green. Records DR-0203, memory feedback_bulk_clean_duplicates.
- **Source:** app/src/lib/learned-dedupe.js

The bottom control keeps the full 30D/90D/ALL/CUSTOM; the top is the month quick-compare only (no confusing duplication). Placed as a normal top-of-content row (not sticky) so it never collides with the fixed FABs; palette + rem sizing reuse approved tokens (no new debt). ### REV-0189 · Curriculum — the course-split: over-slot lessons flow across sessions (DR-0215 §2)
- **Date:** 2026-07-21
- **Surface:** app/src/lib/lesson-flow.js (planSessions + buildLessonArc.sessionPlan) · app/src/components/LessonFlow.jsx (run-of-show banner) · app/src/__tests__/lesson-flow.test.js · app/src/__tests__/lesson-flow-sessions-render.test.jsx
- **Type:** feature
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Implements DR-0215 §2's course mechanism (the tracked NEXT alongside the Story Library). When a lesson's spoken teaching runs longer than the chosen slot, it FLOWS across more than one session -- paced, not cut (Darrell: "adjust the curriculum to the allotted time... don't lose content or context"). `planSessions(module, {slotMinutes})` estimates the spoken teaching length (~140 wpm = SPOKEN_WPM, DR-0215's own measure) and, when it exceeds the slot, groups the prose into N even sessions AT SENTENCE BOUNDARIES -- content-preserving by construction (rejoining the sessions reproduces the teaching verbatim, in order; every word MOVED, never dropped -- the covenant). A slot-sized lesson stays one session; the split reflows with the slot (a longer slot collapses back to one). `buildLessonArc` now returns `arc.sessionPlan`; `LessonFlow.jsx` shows a "Runs across N sessions" banner (with each session's ~minutes) only when over-slot. REALITY-TRACE (P15/DR-0076): measured the live curriculum -- at a 25-min slot the only over-slot Living Lessons NOW are L50 (~34.9 min -> 2 sessions) and L49 (~25.6 -> 2); DR-0215's "L42 ~29 / L50 ~24" estimate was STALE (L42 is ~18.7 min after later edits, L50 grew to ~35 via the Father-of-Lights wave + stories). The mechanism is GENERAL -- it splits whichever lessons actually exceed the slot, at any slot length -- not a hardcoded L42/L50 special-case. Verified: new planSessions tests (proven-to-catch: content-preserving rejoin, sentence-boundary-only splits, slot drives the count, real L50 >=2 sessions, buildLessonArc surfaces the plan) + run-of-show banner render test (shows at 25 min, hidden at 60 min, hidden for a short lesson); contrast + legibility green; full suite 6279 passed; lint clean. Rides PR #993 (the DR-0215 bundle) under `hold` with the Story Library. Content-preservation covenant honored (no teaching re-authored or dropped -- only grouped).
- **Source:** app/src/lib/lesson-flow.js

### REV-0190 · Ways-review (orchestration) — the 2026-07-21 story/curriculum working day
- **Date:** 2026-07-21
- **Surface:** the delivery lane (auto-open-pr / auto-merge / ci / deploy) + the story/curriculum work (PR #991/#992/#993/#994)
- **Type:** orchestration
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Reviewing HOW this day's work ran (DR-0108 mandatory ways-review; DR-0102 one orchestration record per working day), honestly, prompted by Darrell ("Everything has to go through the Ways and documentation foundation correctly, correct? Did our stories?"). What SHIPPED: L51/L52/L53 + KPI income/outputs reports + the parable/story feature (#991), 55 stories re-developed to the ~250-320w bar (#992), the testimony-first Story Library (#993), the course-split (#994). Content documentation was done correctly (DR-0215; REV-0187/0188/0189; PARABLE-AND-TESTIMONY-METHOD.md). FRICTIONS + LESSONS (each an action):
  1. **The `hold` race let a Tier-C surface merge unheld.** #993 (Story Library, Tier C) merged + deployed despite the `hold` label: native auto-merge ARMS at PR-open, and `hold` was applied ~1 min later — `hold` only blocks ARMING, it cannot disarm an already-armed merge. LESSON/ACTION: for any Tier-C change, apply `hold` BEFORE the push (or push as a draft) so it arms held from the start; never rely on labeling a live PR. Darrell chose NOT to fix the lane now (the operating discipline IS the fix). `re-review: 2026-08-21` (revisit if another held PR slips).
  2. **Pushing faster than CI restarted the merge.** On #991, successive pushes each restarted the auto-merge sweep, delaying the merge. LESSON/ACTION: batch content into ONE final push and let CI (~3 min) settle; do not push again until it lands.
  3. **A squash-merge leaves a duplicate commit that conflicts.** After #990/#991/#992 squash-merged, the pre-squash commit lingered on the working branch and produced a "dirty" (conflicting) next PR. LESSON/ACTION: after each squash-merge, `git rebase --onto origin/main <old-merged-commit> <branch>` to drop the already-merged commit before stacking new work (the merged-PR-restart discipline; used successfully for #991/#993/#994).
  4. **A browser-only import broke a node-env test.** story-library.js importing supabase at top level crashed council-chamber-launch.test.js (node env, no `window`) transitively via ChurchLearn. LESSON/ACTION: in a lib whose PURE logic is imported by node-env tests, import browser-only clients (supabase) LAZILY inside the async functions that use them. (Fixed; now covered.)
  WHAT WENT RIGHT: the deterministic gates held (DR-0076) — Darrell's own frame, "I like the over 6000 plus checks, that's the soak to me" — the 6279-test suite + lint + guards + build ARE the soak that let work land on green with confidence; every merge's deploy was verified against the served SHA (DR-0107), and the one lagging deploy (#992) was hand-dispatched immediately. AFFIRMATIONS recorded: the per-lesson quiz is intentional (it tests the Yahweh concepts + context) — format uniformity across the teaching series is by-design, not a diversity defect; the curriculum is genuinely varied where it matters (tone ~50/50 light/solemn, 25 anchor books, lengths skewing short).
- **Source:** docs/reviews/REVIEWS.md

### REV-0192 · Books/Imported — standard reports drill down to the transactions that make each total
- **Date:** 2026-07-21
- **Surface:** app/src/components/Imported.jsx (allIncome + topCategories models + the ALL INCOME / ALL OUTPUTS report render) · app/src/__tests__/imported-render.test.jsx
- **Type:** feature
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell 2026-07-21 (screenshot of the ALL INCOME standard report): "We need to be able to click and see what makes these totals in green... for rigorous data driven reviews... currently I cant sort the system to give me the exact same format report, why?" THE WHY (answered): a standard report is a GROUP-AND-SUM (bucket every deposit by source -> sum -> % share), so "Salary · 54% · +$10,849" is a COMPUTED total, not a row that exists in the data; the Tx sorter only REORDERS flat rows, it never COLLAPSES them into per-source totals — so no sort setting can reproduce the grouped shape (different operation). THE FIX: each green group total is now a clickable, keyboard-accessible button (aria-expanded) that expands to reveal the exact member transactions that sum to it — date · payee · amount, in the same format — and, where category editing is wired, an inline category selector so a mis-filed transaction can be re-filed right there and the report re-groups live. Applied to BOTH ALL INCOME and ALL OUTPUTS. The member transactions are carried on the model (allIncome/topCategories now keep `txns` per group), traceable not painted (Reality-Trace/DR-0076). Verified: new proven-to-catch render test (the group renders as an expandable button with aria-expanded=false; no drill-down <li> before click; after click aria-expanded=true and the member deposits — incl. the $700 payroll — appear as <li>); legibility guard caught a light-on-light hover-bg token which was replaced with hover:underline (no new bg token); imported-render + finance-reports + contrast + legibility green; full suite 6286 passed; lint clean.
- **Source:** app/src/components/Imported.jsx

REALITY-TRACE (P15/DR-0076): the app has NO PDF/document import today — Imported = bank transactions, 1099s = contractor forms, tax-id-vault = SSN digits device-only, Legal.jsx = a placeholder for encrypted PIN-gated documents (not built). So a years-of-returns PDF has no home; a filed return is a sensitive DOCUMENT (SSNs + full financials), not ledger data. BUILT the storage-agnostic SPINE (the part fully verifiable from here; the storage BACKEND location — NAS-sovereign vs RLS-walled cloud bucket — is the one genuine Tier-C decision that is Darrell's, and NAS provisioning is outside this cloud sandbox): tax-documents.js — record shape keyed by YEAR + ENTITY + KIND (return/w2/1099-received/k1/schedule/receipt/other), never-lose validation (rejects a missing year/entity/kind or a non-PDF), deterministic id, groupByYear (newest-year-first, filed return before supporting docs), and fileTaxDoc(doc, bytes, backend) that routes bytes through an INJECTED storage backend (NAS or bucket drops in unchanged) and NEVER fabricates success (a backend failure returns status:'error', storageRef:null). The behavioral-strategy BRIDGE (Darrell's stated purpose): buildTaxHistory(docs) → the DATA side of the Behavioral Mirror (DATA→TRUTH→IDENTITY→INVITATION) — one row per year with figures (grossIncome/agi/totalTax/refund) + year-over-year deltas, REAL NUMBERS ONLY: a year with no verified figures is surfaced 'pending', never invented (DR-0076 — extraction from the PDF is a later independently-verified step; hand-entry is the honest first path). VERIFIED: 15 proven-to-catch tests (validation rejects ambiguous/incomplete + non-PDF; grouping order; fileTaxDoc routes/rejects/reports-failure/skips-no-backend; history deltas + pending-never-invented); lint clean. NEXT (his call to unblock): storage-backend location (recommend NAS-sovereign for SSN-bearing returns), then the Books → Taxes upload surface, then verified figure-extraction. This is groundwork; the surface + backend are Tier C (soak + Governor review).

(2) infra/nas-tax-ingest/tax_ingest.py — deterministic stdlib-only job (authored like imported_snapshot.py): walks /volume1/PoeTech/tax-documents/<entity>/<year>/*.pdf, infers kind from filename, copies each PDF into the Caddy site (served + printable same-origin), reads VERIFIED figures from a <name>.figures.json sidecar (hand-entered + checked, never OCR-guessed -- DR-0076), and writes the light archive.json atomically. (3) README with the paste-ready SSH/ConnectBot run command (DR-0108). This resolves "lighter + reusable + still printable + storage": the app carries only light data + a pointer (reusable for the Behavioral Mirror strategy layer), the heavy original lives on sovereign NAS disk (no cloud bill) and is served for printing. VERIFIED: tax-archive 8 proven-to-catch tests (reads snapshot; returns EMPTY/never-throws on 404/network-error/malformed; printableUrl resolves original / returns null) + tax-documents 15 tests = 23 green; the Python job byte-compiles AND smoke-tests (read a fixture PDF + figures sidecar, inferred kind='return', built the pointer, carried verified figures); lint clean. NEXT (Tier C, his call already given -- NAS-sovereign, "I want it"): the Books -> Taxes upload surface, and sovereign embeddings on the GPU node as a search aid (never the source of a number). Grounds a WAYS-REVIEW: scope to the TEAM's existing capabilities (the NAS pipeline), not the agent's blank-slate reflex.

### REV-0199 · Ways-review (orchestration) — review the governing Ways BEFORE writing architecture (the n8n-vs-Python catch)
- **Date:** 2026-07-21
- **Surface:** docs/decisions/DR-0217 · the app↔NAS bridge framing
- **Type:** orchestration
- **Status:** logged
- **Findings:** Darrell caught it: "Why n8n and not python like our Ways and documentation state?" — then "review our Ways and documentation for clarification" and "Did you know that or do that first thing in your workout routine?" HONEST ways-review (DR-0108/DR-0102): the BUILD followed the sovereign-Python Way (tax_ingest.py stdlib mirroring imported_snapshot.py's "No n8n" replacement; tax_upload_server.py FastAPI; archive.json served by Caddy — zero n8n, aligned with DR-0083). But the FRAMING did not: DR-0217 (first draft) cited `/n8n` as "the bridge every NAS feature rides" — I reality-traced the CODE (found the existing /n8n proxy) but did NOT first review the WAYS (DR-0083 "loops = plain Python on the NAS, not n8n"; DR-0132 "n8n off the reliability-critical path") that are actively retiring n8n. The agent did not surface the inconsistency; the principal did. THE MISS: Reality-Trace + DR-0108 must run against the governing DECISION RECORDS (not just the current code) BEFORE writing new architecture — the code can embody legacy the Ways are retiring, so pattern-matching the code alone can propagate the very thing being deprecated. THE FIX (this REV): (1) DR-0217 corrected to separate TRANSPORT (the Cloudflare Pages Function → Funnel proxy; `/n8n` is a LEGACY name DR-0083 retires; new features get a sovereign-neutral route like `/taxes/*`) from BACKEND (sovereign Python/FastAPI + Caddy, never a new n8n webhook) — the tax pipeline named as the first fully-Python end-to-end example. (2) Standing ways-step recorded: before any DR/architecture, grep the decision ledger for the governing direction (here: DR-0083/DR-0132 on n8n) and reconcile, not just the code. TRACKED improvement (DR-0075): rename the `/n8n` transport route to a sovereign-neutral name so the legacy label stops implying an n8n backend. Grounds the clarification; pairs with DR-0083/DR-0132/DR-0108/DR-0061.
- **Source:** docs/decisions/DR-0217-app-is-the-single-access-surface-nas-is-backup.md
